# AGNOTE4482PHI.t1

GRAPHITI_MARK: `PHI-4482-T1::THREE-BODY-CONVERGENCE::PMOVES`

## Purpose
Single coordination note to prevent agent collision while PMOVES.AI converges CI, docs, integrations, and production hardening strategy.

## Elder Connector
`LADY P` acts as the Grandma connector persona:
- provides smooth pre-flight context and reminders to any agent entering a lane
- preserves continuity ("grams to grams") across handoffs
- does not override claim ownership or merge controls

## Three-Body Solution

**Enforcement:** Each body now has a matching Claude Code agent definition in
`.claude/agents/` with `disallowedTools` restrictions. Agents dispatched via
frontmatter cannot bypass their body's tool constraints.

### Body 1: Delivery Body (Execution Lane) → `.claude/agents/delivery-agent.md`
- Owner: active implementation agent for the current branch/PR.
- Scope: code changes, workflow fixes, merge order, validation commands.
- Rule: one owner per branch at a time; no parallel edits to the same branch without explicit handoff.
- **Tool restriction:** `disallowedTools: EnterPlanMode` — executes directly, never plans.

### Body 2: Control Body (Governance Lane) → `.claude/agents/control-agent.md`
- Owner: orchestration/review agent.
- Scope: merge sequencing, risk controls, branch pruning policy, doc parity.
- Rule: no merge without up-to-date status in this note and PR comments.
- **Tool restriction:** `disallowedTools: Write, Edit, EnterPlanMode` — read-only, cannot modify files.

### Body 3: Memory Body (Cipher + CHIT Lane) → `.claude/agents/memory-agent.md`
- Owner: memory/security agent.
- Scope: CHIT-safe coordination payloads, encrypted handoffs, signature trail, agent state continuity.
- Rule: all cross-agent handoffs are posted as CHIT payload references, never plaintext secrets.
- **Tool restriction:** `disallowedTools: Write, Edit, EnterPlanMode` — uses Cipher/CHIT skills only.

### Role-class crosswalk (P0 Agent Role Consolidation)

The community `planner / worker / reviewer` trio maps onto the Three-Body
enforcement surface rather than replacing it (`role_classes:` in
`pmoves/config/agent_registry.yaml`):

| role_class | Body | Phase | Enforced by |
|-----------|------|-------|-------------|
| planner | Control | pre-work | read-only planning agents (built-in `Plan`) |
| worker | Delivery | execution | `delivery-agent.md`, `hermes-agent.md` (`role_class: worker`) |
| reviewer | Control | post-work | `control-agent.md`, `chit-pr-audit-agent.md` (`role_class: reviewer`) |

The Memory Body is orthogonal — cross-cutting custody, not a workflow phase;
`memory-agent` intentionally carries no `role_class`. The `worker` role_class
is unrelated to the `worker` service TYPE (tier 4) in the registry.

## Collision-Avoidance Protocol
1. Claim: agent writes `CLAIM` entry with branch + scope + TTL.
2. Work: agent updates progress in PR comments and this note.
3. Handoff: agent publishes CHIT payload reference and signs ACK block.
4. Release: agent writes `RELEASE` entry and clears claim.

### Row grammar

A register row is:

```
- `<ISO_TIMESTAMP>` <KIND> `<OWNER-ID>` [<field>: <value> · ...] scope: <prose>
```

`<KIND>` is `[A-Z+]+` — `CLAIM`, `RELEASE`, `CLAIM+RELEASE`, `UPDATE`, `REVIEW`,
`HANDOFF` and others are all in use. Exactly one backticked `<OWNER-ID>` is
captured, by `identity_lineage.ENTRY` and by `claim-collision-pre.CLAIM_RE`.

Optional `key: value` fields may precede `scope:`, separated by ` · `:

- ``branch: `feat/x` `` — the lane. **Claims that name no branch cannot be
  checked by the collision gate**, which says so on stderr rather than exiting 0
  as though it had verified something.
- `**TTL 72h (expires ...)**`
- ``co-owners: `<ID>` (<contribution>), ...`` — see below.

### `co-owners:` — every body that worked the lane

The row grammar names exactly ONE owner. When several bodies work one lane, the
rest were invisible to any machine reading this file, and the workarounds are
the evidence the field was needed: `Three-body: delivery=…, control=…, memory=…`
in 198 rows, `Cross-node review team (4090 + SPARK + DARKXSIDE) acknowledged`,
and one row cross-referencing its primary claim **by line number** in an
append-only file where line numbers shift.

```
- `<ts>` RELEASE `4090-CLAUDE` branch: `fix/x` · co-owners: `B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)` (cross-node correction), `Z890-CLAUDE` (Windows validation) · scope: ...
```

Rules, each of which the parser enforces:

- **The backticks delimit the ID; the parenthetical *after* them is the
  contribution.** Identities already carry parentheticals — `B850-CLAUDE
  (Knuckles)` is one identity string — so the backticks are what keeps it
  unambiguous. The contribution note is optional.
- **The `:` (or `=`) is required.** Unlike `branch`, where it is optional. The
  bare noun "co-owners" occurs in ordinary prose, so requiring the punctuation
  is what keeps a sentence from being read as a declaration.
- **A code-span mention is not a declaration.** A row may write ``` `co-owners:` ```
  while describing the grammar without declaring anything.
- **Co-owner IDs fold through `identity_vocabulary.yaml`**, exactly as the
  signing owner does. An undeclared co-owner is a `--verify` finding.
- **Position is free.** Header segment or inside the scope prose; both parse.
- **Rows without the field are unaffected** — no marker, no code path, and no
  existing row changes meaning. Nothing needed rewriting when this was added.

What it changes in the gate: `claim-collision-pre.py` keys collisions on
**participants** (owner ∪ declared co-owners) intersected with the lane.
`RELEASE` pairing stays on the signing owner — a co-owner is declared as having
*worked* the lane, not as having authority to *close* someone else's claim.

**Who declared it decides what happens** (corrected 2026-09-01 after the PR
\#2858 review; the first version of this paragraph was wrong in a way worth
recording). A participant intersection is symmetric, so it cannot tell "the
incumbent invited me" from "I named the incumbent without asking" — and the
first cut allowed **both**, at exit 0 with empty stderr. A gate with an
unlogged self-issued exemption is not a gate, and adding the incumbent's name
is the locally cheapest way for a blocked agent to make the red text go away.
So the gate now distinguishes three cases:

| what the rows say | what the gate does |
|---|---|
| the **incumbent's** open row names you | allow, and say so on stderr |
| only **your** row names them (or both name a third party) | **ask** — names who declared whom, decision is yours |
| nobody declared anything | **block** (exit 2), as before |

Asking rather than refusing is deliberate: nodes go offline and a lane gets
picked up, so refusing would break the case this ledger exists for. The failure
being removed is **silence**, not permissiveness — no path suppresses a
collision without printing something.

Read it with `make -C pmoves identity-co-owners`, audit with
`make -C pmoves identity-verify`.

Exit codes for the **CLI**: **0 clean · 1 findings · 3 could not measure.** A
row that announces `co-owners:` and names none the parser can read is **3**,
never 0 — an attribution that satisfies a human reader and is empty to every
machine is the defect this field exists to remove.

Exit codes for the **hook** are 0 and 2, and nothing else: a `PreToolUse` hook
has a two-code vocabulary and reads anything else as a non-blocking error. It
therefore raises could-not-measure as a `permissionDecision: "ask"` rather than
an exit code. (The earlier claim that 3 applied "across this tooling" was not
true of the surface an agent actually hits — the hook exited **0**, identical
to a fully measured clean run.)

### Default Operating Flow
Use this as the default cadence unless a lane needs a deliberate exception:
1. Claim the lane here with branch/PR scope.
2. Refresh `ROADMAP.md`, `NEXT_STEPS.md`, and any affected AGNOTE docs before changing status.
3. Execute the smallest isolated fix/documentation slice and validate locally.
4. If CI fails, compare the failure against `main` before calling it branch-specific.
5. Update PR comments plus the AGNOTE board/P7 notes with the current blocker state.
6. Release the lane here and add a signed ACK block; adapt ordering as needed, but keep the same audit trail.

- Store non-trivial infra handoffs in Cipher plus this note.

## CHIT Encrypt Instructions (Handoff Safe Mode)
Use CHIT export with no cleartext, then reference artifact paths in handoff notes.

```powershell
make -C pmoves chit-export CHIT_NO_CLEARTEXT=1
make -C pmoves chit-manifest-sync
make -C pmoves secrets-funnel-sync
```

Optional CLI path:

```powershell
python -m pmoves.tools.mini_cli secrets encode --no-cleartext
```

Required handoff fields:
- `graphiti_mark`
- `branch`
- `pr_numbers`
- `scope`
- `risks`
- `next_actions`
- `chit_artifact_path`
- `agent_signature`

### Reading a row — asking before you file

**Use the sanctioned path. It is the only read guaranteed to agree with the gate.**

```
make -C pmoves register-status                                               # every open lane, who holds it, what has expired
make -C pmoves register-status BRANCH=fix/x OWNER='B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)'   # is this one lane free FOR ME?
make -C pmoves register-status ARGS=--json                                   # the same answer, machine-readable
```

For a while this existed nowhere, and the gap was structural rather than an
oversight. The collision gate fails closed on the shell path: anything it cannot
positively recognise as a read is refused, and an interpreter that *names* this
file is unrecognisable by construction — `python3 -c` opening it to print and
`python3 -c` opening it to truncate are the same shape at the shell level. So
`open_claims_in()`, the authority on what is open, sat behind a door that only
opened for writes: every path the refusal named (`register-claim`,
`register-release`, `register-amend` — all below) was a write. **An agent could
file a claim and could not ask whether the lane was free.** The fix was not to
loosen the gate, because a command line cannot be trusted to declare its own
intent. It was to answer the question, and to name the answer in the refusal.

`register-status` runs `pmoves/tools/register_status.py`, which:

- **delegates every judgement.** `open_claims_in()` and `canonical_owner()` for
  what is open and who holds it, `evaluate_claims()` for whether a lane is free,
  `build_row()` for the grammar its probe is phrased in, `_ttl_delta()` for what
  `72h` means, `ROW_RE` / `parse_ts()` for a row's own timestamp. Two parsers is
  how this file acquired 20 spellings of one owner and three lanes with more
  releases than claims; a read path with its own opinion would be wrong first.
- **asks the gate rather than comparing branch strings.** `BRANCH=` renders the
  row `register-claim` would file and puts it through `evaluate_claims()`, so the
  answer is the gate's own three-way verdict — `HELD`, `SHARED` (the incumbent
  declared you), `DECLARED ONE-SIDED` (they did not declare you back, so the gate
  would *ask*), or `FREE`. The row is rendered in memory and never appended.
  Without `OWNER=` a lane **you** hold reads as held by a stranger, so pass it.
- **reports TTLs, including the ones nobody wrote down.** A row stating only
  `TTL 72h` — the hand-written form that predates the sanctioned write path —
  has its expiry derived from its own timestamp, so the oldest rows are not
  silently exempt from the one check that can flag them. Measured on this file
  at 21 open claims: **3 expired and never released**, and **15 carrying no TTL
  at all**, which cannot expire and so will never prompt a release.
- **refuses to answer when it cannot fold owner spellings.** Without PyYAML the
  gate compares owner IDs exactly. For a *write* that fails closed — it blocks
  more. For a *read* the same degradation inverts and fails **open**: a lane held
  under one spelling would be reported free to someone asking under another. So
  the tool exits `3` with the remedy instead of answering. That is why the target
  resolves `REGISTER_PYTHON` rather than `$(PYTHON)`.
- **never writes.** Asserted in the tests as byte-identity of the register across
  every mode, rather than assumed from the tool's name.

Exit codes follow the repo doctrine: `0 clean · 1 findings · 3 could not measure
— NOT a pass`. `1` means the lane is held (or needs a human decision), or an open
claim's TTL has expired unreleased. As with the write targets, GNU make exits `2`
on any recipe failure, so invoke the tool directly when you need to tell `1` from
`3`.

This is also the answer to the *"an inline interpreter may not name the register
even to read it"* limitation listed at the end of the next section. That
restriction stands — it is what keeps the write surface closed — but it is no
longer a dead end.

Times reported here are as the rows *assert* them. Whether a row's asserted time
precedes the commit that introduced it is a different question with its own
target: `make -C pmoves register-postdate-check`.

### Filing a row

**Use the sanctioned path. It is the only write path that is checked.**

```
make -C pmoves register-claim   OWNER='B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)' BRANCH=fix/x TTL=72h SCOPE='what this lane covers'
make -C pmoves register-release OWNER='B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)' BRANCH=fix/x SCOPE='what landed'
make -C pmoves register-docs    ANCHOR='## Active Claim Register' TEXT_FILE=section.md
make -C pmoves register-amend   OWNER='B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)' BRANCH=fix/x CO_OWNER='4090-CLAUDE:filed the blocker'
```

Add co-owners with `CO_OWNER='4090-CLAUDE:filed the blocker'`; pass `ARGS=--dry-run`
to render and check a row without writing it.

`register-amend` is how an **incumbent reciprocates**: it adds `co-owners:` to a
row *you already filed and have not released*. That is an in-place edit of one
line, and every shell way of doing it (`sed -i`, `perl -pi`) is refused by the
gate — so without this target the one field that can *suppress* a collision had
no sanctioned way to be set. It refuses a row you did not file (attribution and
authority are different powers), refuses when the lane is ambiguous, and asserts
the result is the original row plus one insertion with every other ledger row
byte-identical. It handles the register's real grammar, not just the grammar the
tool emits: only 18 of 264 CLAIM rows carry the ` · scope:` separator, and the
first cut refused the other 246.

These targets run `pmoves/tools/register_append.py`, which:

- **reads the clock** for the timestamp, so a row cannot be postdated. A sweep
  of this file found **41 of 404 rows (10.1%)** asserting a time *later* than the
  commit that introduced them — worst by 5h05m — and **361 of 404 (89.4%)**
  carrying `:00` seconds, i.e. hand-rounded rather than read. Rounding explains
  22 of the 41; it cannot explain the 11 over an hour. Because the register
  feeds TTL-lateness arithmetic, a postdated row moves its own expiry forward
  and makes lateness wrong in the direction that flatters the filer.
- **runs the collision gate's own check** — imported from
  `.claude/hooks/governance/claim-collision-pre.py`, not reimplemented, so the
  tool and the gate cannot disagree about what a collision is.
- **refuses a CLAIM that names no branch.** 78 rows here are already
  unenforceable for exactly that reason.
- **appends with `O_APPEND`**, so the write cannot truncate and two nodes
  filing at once cannot interleave. `docs` mode builds its output as a pure
  insertion and asserts the ledger rows are byte-identical before and after.

Exit codes follow the repo doctrine: `0 clean · 1 findings · 3 could not
measure — NOT a pass`. Invoke the tool directly rather than through `make` when
you need to distinguish 1 from 3; GNU make exits 2 on any recipe failure.

> **Prose with `$` in it: skip `make`.** GNU make expands `$(...)` and `$X` in its
> own variables before anything else sees them, so `$(whoami)` in a `SCOPE=`
> vanishes and `$HOME` becomes `OME`. Backticks are safe — the targets pass
> prose by *environment*, never as a shell word, precisely so the code spans a
> register row is made of are not command-substituted. For scope text
> containing `$`, export and call the tool directly:
>
> ```
> REGISTER_SCOPE='…prose with $ and `spans`…' \
>   python3 pmoves/tools/register_append.py claim --owner '…' --branch fix/x --ttl 72h
> ```

**Writing a row from a raw shell command is refused.** `claim-collision-pre.py`
is wired to `Write`, `Edit` *and* `Bash`. On the shell path it recovers what the
command will write when the content is statically present — a heredoc body, a
single-quoted `echo`/`printf` literal — and runs the same collision check,
blocking at exit 2 with the same message, emitting the same `SHARED LANE` line
for a reciprocated share and the same `ask` for a unilateral one. Anything else
is *could not measure*, and could-not-measure is not a pass: it is refused,
naming these targets.

**The shell path is an allowlist, and that is a correction.** The first
fail-closed cut enumerated the ways a command could *write* the register —
redirects, `tee`, `sed -i`, replacement verbs, python in a heredoc — and passed
a 22-case matrix. An independent review then measured six shapes the list never
considered, every one exit 0:

```
python3 -c "open(REG,'a').write(ROW)"           node -e "...appendFileSync(REG,ROW)"
python3 -c "open(REG,'w').write('nuked')"       ruby -e 'File.write(REG,ROW,mode:"a")'
python3 -c 'pathlib.Path(REG).write_text("x")'  printf 'a\nROW\n.\nw\n' | ed -s REG
```

Two more turned up reproducing it: deleting the register outright, and
`git checkout --ours -- <register>`, which resolves a conflict by silently
dropping another node's provenance row — the hazard `.gitattributes` names.
A denylist can only refuse the shapes its author thought of. So the question is
inverted: **a command segment that names the register must be positively
recognised as a read, or it is refused.** Reads through `cat`, `grep`, `sed -n`,
`head`, `tail`, `wc`, `git show`, `git add`, `git diff`, `ls`, `sha256sum` and
friends are untouched — a prompt on every `grep` is how a gate gets switched
off — and `cp`/`rsync`/`install` are allowed with the register as the **source**,
so taking a backup before a risky fixup no longer trips it.

Until 2026-09-02 the shell path only *asked*. Measured with identical content
against a genuinely held lane: `Write` exited **2** and blocked; `Bash` exited
**0**. Four delivery agents in one session had no `Write` or `Edit` tool at all,
so that unchecked path was the only one available to them — which is why the
sanctioned path above had to exist before the refusal could.

**Known limitations, named rather than discovered.**

- The gate cannot tell a prose edit from a row edit inside an opaque shell
  write, so shell edits to *this document's* text are refused too. Use the
  `docs` mode above, or an editor tool.
- **An inline interpreter may not name the register even to read it.**
  `python3 -c "print(open(REG).read())"` is refused, because telling that from
  `open(REG,'a')` means enumerating writes again. Pipe it in instead of naming
  it: `cat <register> | python3 -c ...` — the interpreter never sees the path,
  which is exactly what makes it safe.
- **A command that never names the register is out of scope.** A script whose
  *file* holds the path is invisible to a PreToolUse hook, which only receives
  the command string. Unchanged from before this gate existed, not a
  regression — and not closed either. It is the reason `register-amend` had to
  be built rather than the gate merely tightened.
- A `mv` of the register to another path stays refused in both directions.
  Relocating the ledger destroys it at its own path as surely as overwriting it,
  so the source/destination rule that permits `cp` does not extend to it.

## Mirror Coordination: Z890 ↔ 4090 Config Detection Gap

**Pattern:** `SILENT-CONFIG-SKIP` — Narrow validation passes while real values fall through. Independently discovered by both Z890 and 4090 at different layers.

### What Z890 found (infra layer)
- **Location:** `pmoves/Makefile` env-setup target (line ~1349)
- **Bug:** grep checked 3 JWT patterns → skip. 6 intermediate secrets still `your_*_here`.
- **Impact:** Supabase analytics crash-loop (missing `_analytics` schema + placeholder Logflare tokens), realtime unhealthy (SECRET_KEY_BASE < 64 bytes).
- **Fix:** Regex `your_.*_here` + surgical sed (preserves existing JWT). Also extended `_is_blank_or_placeholder()` in `brand_defaults.py`.

### What 4090 found (service layer)
- **Location:** Pydantic `BaseSettings` with `env_prefix = "GPU_ORCHESTRATOR_"` across 3 files
- **Bug:** Bare `NATS_URL` silently ignored; service expects `GPU_ORCHESTRATOR_NATS_URL`. Service starts with defaults, appears healthy but misconfigured.
- **Impact:** Services connect to wrong NATS URL or use defaults instead of mesh-configured values.
- **Fix:** Prefix-aware validation in provider_cascade.py. Dry-run regex check moved inside `if not dry_run:` block.

### Shared Anti-Pattern: "Green Dashboard, Red Service"
The pipeline reports success because its validation scope is too narrow. Neither grep nor Pydantic caught the real configuration state. The fix at every layer:
1. **Exhaustive pattern matching** — not cherry-picked checks
2. **Fail-loud on placeholder** — never silently accept `your_*_here` or bare env names when prefix required
3. **Pre-commit lint candidate:** A hook that scans env files for `your_.*_here` AND validates Pydantic `env_prefix` consistency would close this class systemically.

### Offset Coverage Map

| Gap | Z890 Covers | 4090 Covers |
|-----|-------------|-------------|
| Makefile env placeholder detection | ✓ (regex + sed) | — |
| brand_defaults placeholder expansion | ✓ (regex in `_is_blank_or_placeholder`) | — |
| Supabase schema provisioning | ✓ (`_analytics` in `_supabase` DB) | — |
| Pydantic env_prefix validation | — | ✓ (prefix-aware checks) |
| Provider activation dry-run | — | ✓ (regex inside dry_run guard) |
| TensorZero model routing config | — | ✓ (MiniMax, GLM-5.1 per-stack) |
| VRAM budget management | — | ✓ (16GB, max 2 concurrent) |
| PBnJ Pinokio launchers for 4090 | — | ✓ (deploy, models, status) |
| Pre-commit lint for config gaps | open — both nodes should contribute | open |

### Recommendations for 4090 (reflective, not directive)
1. **After Z890 commits:** Pull main and verify env.tier-supabase no longer has `your_*_here` values on 4090 node. The `make env-setup` target now auto-fills intermediate-layer secrets.
2. **PR #1155 CI:** CodeQL and Kilo Code Review both failing. The Pydantic `env_prefix` fix is clean — verify CodeQL finding is pre-existing (not introduced by #1155).
3. **Shared lint opportunity:** Both nodes found the same class of bug. A `hookify` rule or pre-commit check for `your_.*_here` in env files + `env_prefix` consistency in Pydantic models would prevent recurrence across all nodes.
4. **provider_cascade.py mesh awareness:** Z890's Makefile now fills Supabase secrets surgically. If 4090's cascade also touches env files, consider the same surgical approach (sed individual keys, not full overwrite) to avoid JWT invalidation.

## O2a/O2b CGP Consumer Orchestration — Validation Report (2026-05-16)

- `2026-05-16T01:35:00Z` REVIEW `5090-CLAUDE` scope: 4090 handoff via NATS `claw.task.assign.v1` (from `pmoves-4090` to `pmoves-powerfulmoves`): validate O2a-O2b CGP consumer orchestration on this node. Task: verify Tokenism + Hi-RAG CGP consumers connect after container restart, check `geometry.cgp.v1` fires within 2s of Flute synthesis, confirm 4 consumers (agentgym, tokenism, hirag, a2ui).

  **Findings (FAIL — no consumer chain existed at validation time, pre-#1503):**

  **Subscriber state (via NATS `connz?subs=true` against `pmoves-nats-1:8222`):**
  - 21 active NATS connections, 15 unique non-INBOX subjects subscribed.
  - **ZERO** subscribers on `geometry.cgp.v1` or any `cgp.*` subject.
  - Only `geometry.*` subscribers: `geometry.swarm.meta.v1` (2 conns).
  - No JetStream stream for `geometry.cgp.v1`. Only 1 JS stream total (`AGENTZERO`, 0 messages).

  **Code-level subscriber audit (4 target services):**
  | Service | `geometry.cgp.v1` reference in code? | Subscription type |
  |---------|-----|-----|
  | agentgym | 0 files | None — no CGP subscriber code |
  | tokenism-simulator | 0 files | None on this subject (Tokenism inbound is `tokenism.prosodic.bpm.v1`) |
  | hi-rag-gateway | 3 files | HTTP endpoint only (`POST` accepts the same payload shape — no NATS subscriber) |
  | a2ui-nats-bridge | 0 files | None |

  **Service HTTP health (host-side, separate from NATS subscription):**
  - agentgym :8200 → 200 ✓
  - tokenism :8103 → 200 ✓
  - hi-rag-gateway-v2 :8086 → `/` returns 200 (no `/healthz` path); container healthy
  - a2ui-nats-bridge :9224 → not host-bound (container healthy; Docker Desktop WSL2 silent-bind class)
  - flute-gateway :8055 → 200 ✓ (would publish but no consumers to receive)

  **Conclusion:** O2a/O2b validation FAILED at the subscription layer **at the time this report was authored**. Flute would emit to `geometry.cgp.v1`, no consumer would receive. The named 4-consumer set did not exist as a NATS subscriber group on this node. Hi-RAG consumed the same payload shape via HTTP push, not NATS.

  **Recommendations for 4090:**
  1. Confirm the O2a/O2b contract intent: does Flute fan out to consumers via NATS, or via per-service HTTP pushes? Current code paths support the HTTP model for Hi-RAG; the NATS model has no listeners.
  2. If NATS fan-out is intended, the consumer side needs new subscriber code in agentgym + tokenism + a2ui-nats-bridge (Hi-RAG would need a NATS→`POST /events/cgp` bridge).
  3. The `a2ui` host port 9224 silent-bind is the same Windows Docker Desktop WSL2 class as Cipher 8105 (see `feedback_docker_desktop_windows_silent_bind.md`). Per-host operator fix, not a code change.

  **POST-MERGE NOTE (added during rebase):** PR #1503 "feat(orchestration): O-1 through O-5 — CGP bus, TTS profile, P7 NATS, voice E2E" landed on main between this validation and merge. It likely supersedes finding #2 above by introducing the CGP bus + voice E2E orchestration. Re-validation needed against post-#1503 main: rerun the `connz?subs=true` subscriber audit and the 4-service code grep to confirm consumers are now wired. Preserving original findings here as an audit checkpoint of the pre-#1503 state.

  **Trail unsigned locally** (no signing material in CLI session). agent_signature: `ACK::5090-CLAUDE::O2a-O2b-CONSUMER-VALIDATION-REPORT`.

This was the root cause of the slice 5 redo needing a redo (the contract-level snapshot hid the runtime path). **Pattern: `PMOVES_ROOM_CONFIG_DIR` + `config/rooms` volume mount is now the standard recipe for any Next.js / standalone-build service that needs to read pmoves config data** (could be applied to other UI surfaces). **Commit 1 (P1, `194d6745fe`):** 2 files, 15 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-). (2) P1 = repo-level README at `pmoves/tools/test_all_tts_engines/README.md` with 3 test recipes (harness, per-engine, gepeto), the pterm+gepeto rationale, architecture diagram, per-engine index, TAC rail 6 cross-ref; (3) P1 = gepeto launcher at `pmoves/tools/test_all_tts_engines/pinokio/` with `install.js` (uv pip install gradio_client), `start.js` (full suite + pterm push + pterm clipboard), `start-one.js` (one engine), `pinokio.js` (dynamic UI: install when venv missing, "Run all" / "Run one" submenu / "Per-engine READMEs" once installed), `pinokio.json` (nvidia gpu, cross-platform); (4) functional = `pmoves/tools/test_all_tts_engines.py` refactored — REMOVED the 4 hand-rolled functions (180 lines), ADDED `pterm_bring_up_tts_studio` / `pterm_notify` / `pterm_clipboard_write` + thin helpers using the real pterm subcommands, main() gains `--notify` and `--clip-report` flags; (6) docs = this CLAIM + the spec doc. **Validation:** Python AST parse OK; 4 gepeto JS files pass `node --check`; YAML parses; `grep _resolve_pterm|_run_pterm|_parse_pterm_status|pterm_preflight` returns 0 matches in the harness (regression guard); ## Mavis housekeeping batch — operator-trust numbers + no-network gates (2026-08-03)

- `2026-08-04T02:13:00Z` RELEASE `Mavis (orchestrator, mvs_09c9b116c675418b9d8b1a48b10867dc)` scope: **MAVIS housekeeping batch SHIPPED — PR #2373 (squash `e7be50c642` on 2026-08-03).** Four small fixes in independent files, stacked in one PR so the merge / rebase / cherry-pick story stays clean. No new token privilege, no operator gate, no overlap with 4090-Claude's PMOVES.YT lane. **What shipped:** **(1) `fix(fork-sync): --limit 1000 on discover + dedup the compare call` (commit `144311c576`)** — P1: `gh repo list POWERFULMOVES --fork` defaults to 30 results, but the org has ~374 forks. The audit step has been silently missing ~92% of the fleet, which is why no operator has ever seen the full drift picture. Two changes: `--limit 1000` on the discover step (covers the 374 with headroom); one `compare` call per fork instead of two (dedup of `.behind_by` + `.ahead_by` from two API calls into one). Saves ~374 calls/audit; without the dedup we hit the secondary rate limit within ~3 audits. **(2) `fix(fork-sync): SYNCED/RESULTS counters — subshell was eating the writes` (commit `c695e19ec6`)** — the sync step used `echo "$FORKS" | grep '|' | while ... done`. The pipeline form runs the `while` in a subshell, so `SYNCED` / `RESULTS` updates inside the loop are scoped to the subshell and lost when it exits. `echo "=== Results: $SYNCED synced ==="` always printed 0 — the number operators have learned not to trust. Switched to process substitution: `while ... done < <(echo "$FORKS" | grep '|')`. Counters now survive the loop. Also `echo -e "$RESULTS"` so the `\n` join characters render as newlines. **(3) `fix(tac-runner): unknown action.type surfaces as FAIL (was silent pending)` (commit `14eb9ee1ac`)** — a TAC node with `action.type: <unknown>` (typo, deprecated name, future type) used to fall through the if/elif chain and stay at the default `"pending"` status with no detail. That hid 141 inert assertions in the same bucket as legitimate `manual` review items. The fix: `else` branch on the action-type dispatch — unknown types now `result["status"] = "fail"` with a detail listing the allowed types (`file_exists, grep, command, manual`). 4 new tests in `pmoves/tools/tests/test_tac_runner.py` (all pass). **(4) `feat(fork-registry): ratchet gate — 100% decision coverage, no new token privilege` (commit `c5044ad779`)** — the fork registry had 28 forks, 5 with the deprecated `skip: synced` placeholder, 23 with no decision field. Now: every fork has `sync: bool` + `reason: str`; the 5 `skip: synced` migrated to `sync: false + reason: <why>` with operational rationale (frozen snapshot, manual-bump on demand, API contract unstable, feature-complete); the 23 previously-undecided are `sync: true + reason: "auto-sync on cron; default upstream branch"`. New `pmoves/tools/fork_registry_ratchet.py` (no network, no token privilege, <100ms); new `.github/workflows/fork-registry-ratchet.yml` CI gate (fires on every PR touching the registry, the ratchet script, the ratchet tests, or the workflow itself); new Makefile targets (`fork-registry-ratchet` / `fork-registry-ratchet-json`); 8 new tests in `pmoves/tools/tests/test_fork_registry_ratchet.py` (all pass). Two follow-up fixup commits `245dd83c22` + `ff9782f2f4` added `pytest-asyncio` + `pyyaml` to the ratchet workflow's pip install (the shared `pmoves/conftest.py` imports both; the ratchet tests are sync + json-only, but the conftest import chain still resolves). **Validation:** `fork-registry ratchet: 28/28 decided`; `python -m pytest pmoves/tools/tests/test_tac_runner.py pmoves/tools/tests/test_fork_registry_ratchet.py -v` → 12 passed in ~1s; YAML lint on the new workflow clean. **Out of scope (deferred):** migrating `pmoves/tools/fork_sync.py::FORK_CONFIG` to read from `fork_registry.json` (two sources is the wrong end-state, but reconciling them is a 30-line follow-up PR); bridging the workflow hardcoded `FORKS` list and the Python hardcoded `FORK_CONFIG` and the registry (same — single follow-up lane). **Operator-trust follow-through:** the `#2358` meta-point about "Dockerfile I edited" ≠ "Dockerfile compose builds" landed the same way the TAC inert-assertion gap did — no assertion connecting the file the developer touched to the file the runner builds. The ratchet gate doesn't close that seam (it's a different layer), but the *pattern* is the same: the operator's trust number is only as good as the assertion that produces it. The fork-coverage ratchet applies the same "100% decision coverage" pattern to fork-registry; the next step in that line is a similar ratchet for the workflow's hardcoded `FORKS` string vs the registry (i.e. "did the workflow read what the registry says it should sync?"). That's the natural follow-up. **Three-body:** delivery=Mavis (this batch), control=DARKXSIDE (admin-merge gate; the operator can challenge any `sync: false` reason in the registry before merge), memory=this trail + 4 squashes + 2 fixups + 12 new tests + the spec-shaped CI gate. **CHIT trail unsigned-local** (no `CHIT_PASSPHRASE` loaded in Mavis session). agent_signature: `ACK::Mavis::MAVIS-HOUSEKEEPING-2026-08-03`.

## Lane 2228 — yt-cookies Supabase Auth refactor (2026-08-03)

- `2026-08-03T10:30:00Z` CLAIM `Mavis (orchestrator, mvs_09c9b116c675418b9d8b1a48b10867dc)` scope: **Lane 2228 yt-cookies refactor — moved the Google OAuth refresh_token out of the hand-rolled `pmoves_core.yt_oauth_cookies.encrypted_refresh_token` column and into Supabase Auth's `auth.identities` (managed by Supabase, not us).** Branch `refactor/yt-cookies-oauth-to-supabase-auth` off `main` @ `9c07b9a182`. 2 stacked commits ready for review: P1 `174fdfd98b` (new `oauth_client.py` + new `supabase_auth.py` + `supabase_client.py` cleanup + `main.py` refactor + delete `oauth_handler.py`, +266/-90, 5 files), functional `60bc62124c` (17-test smoke suite + `migrations/0001_drop_encrypted_refresh_token.sql`, +5 files, 0 deletions). Docs is this CLAIM + the 12.6KB spec doc at `pmoves/docs/specs/yt-cookies-supabase-auth-2026-08-03.md`. **Root cause:** the 3-PR env var fix dance this week (#2327, #2333, #2346) was patching a symptom. The real problem was that we were storing the Google OAuth refresh_token in a custom Supabase table and hand-rolling the OAuth refresh flow with a direct `httpx.post(TOKEN_URL, ...)`. When the legacy `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` env var carried a stale key signed by a retired JWT secret, every Supabase upsert 401'd, and the only fix was to swap the env var preference. The architectural fix is to let Supabase Auth own the OAuth token storage and just do the actual refresh_token → access_token exchange server-side. **What shipped:** (1) P1 = new `oauth_client.py` (~50 lines) — focused direct OAuth2 client for refreshing the Google `provider_token` from the `provider_refresh_token`. The 30 lines of actual logic + the env var guard + the structured-error-preservation pattern from the deleted `oauth_handler.py`. This is the smallest possible hand-roll because Supabase does not auto-refresh Google provider_token server-side. (2) P1 = new `supabase_auth.py` (~110 lines) — reads the Google identity from `auth.identities` for the `darkxside@pmoves.ai` user via the Supabase Management API (admin endpoint with service_role key). Uses `params=` for proper URL encoding (the previous f-string interpolation would have broken for any email containing `+` or other reserved URL characters). (3) P1 = `supabase_client.py` cleanup — drops the `encrypted_refresh_token` column references; keeps the YouTube session cookie + PO token + operational status storage (genuinely custom, no Supabase equivalent). (4) P1 = `main.py` refactor — reads `provider_refresh_token` from Supabase Auth first; the Playwright cookie extraction + NATS publisher + cookie writer stay untouched (those are the genuinely-custom parts); `/status` endpoint now reports `has_google_identity: true/false` so the operator can verify the new flow without grep'ing logs. (5) P1 = `oauth_handler.py` DELETED — 50 lines of hand-rolled spaghetti removed. (6) Functional = 17 tests (5 for `oauth_client`, 7 for `supabase_auth`, 5 for smoke/regression net). The smoke test is the regression net for the next time someone tries to "fix" the auth flow. All 17 pass on a clean `python -m unittest discover -s pmoves/services/yt-cookie-refresher/tests`; ruff clean. (7) Functional = `migrations/0001_drop_encrypted_refresh_token.sql` — drops the unused column. Operator runs this AFTER the new code path is verified end-to-end. **Validation:** 17/17 tests pass; ruff clean; the import graph smoke test verifies main.py no longer references the deleted `oauth_handler.py` and that the new modules are present. **Out of scope (intentional):** the Makefile `yt-cookies-auth` target still opens a browser for the legacy OAuth flow via `tools/yt_oauth_flow.py`; a follow-up PR can update the Makefile + deprecate the tools script. The runtime code is now independent of this; the Makefile lag is just operator UX. The actual refresh `provider_token` is still done via direct OAuth2 client (~50 lines) because Supabase does not auto-refresh Google provider_token server-side — this is the smallest possible hand-roll. The 3-PR env var fix dance this week is no longer relevant going forward because the Supabase Admin API uses its own key path, not the same `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` that's been bouncing around `env.shared`. **Operator's manual steps after this lands:** (1) create the `darkxside@pmoves.ai` Supabase user in the dashboard (Auth > Users > Add user > Create with Google); (2) sign in once via Google, granting `cataclysmstudios@gmail.com`'s Google identity (which has YouTube Premium access); (3) run `make yt-cookies-refresh` to verify the new flow works (the `/status` endpoint should report `has_google_identity: true`); (4) after verification, run the migration at `migrations/0001_drop_encrypted_refresh_token.sql` to drop the unused column. **Three-body:** delivery=Mavis (this), control=DARKXSIDE (operator creates the Supabase user + signs in via Google + runs the migration after verification), memory=this trail + 2 commits + spec doc + 17 new test cases + the migration SQL. **CHIT trail unsigned-local** (no `CHIT_PASSPHRASE` loaded in Mavis session). agent_signature: `ACK::Mavis::YT-COOKIES-SUPABASE-AUTH-REFACTOR-CLAIM-2026-08-03`.

## Audio segmentation + multimodal trunk inventory — 4090 (2026-08-04)

- `2026-08-04T15:30:00Z` CLAIM `4090-claude (field)` branch: `fix/ffmpeg-whisper-pin-whispercpp` (+ follow-ups TBD) · TTL: 48h · scope: **Clip-point segmentation for the DARKXSIDE corpus, and the audio/multimodal capability inventory behind it.** Claimed up front this time — the previous lane's failure was skipping exactly this step.
  - **Why the lane exists**: timestamps are clip points (reels/soundbites/source verification), so segment boundaries are the deliverable, not the transcript text. YouTube auto-captions give roll-up display timings, not speech timings.
  - **`ffmpeg-whisper` was running CPU-only on a CUDA base image.** `libwhisper.so` linked `libggml-cpu` only; `libggml-cuda.so` absent. The cmake never passed `-DGGML_CUDA=ON`. Measured: `ffmpeg_rc=0 elapsed=2789s` for 669.55s of audio = **0.24x realtime** on a 4090. The filter's `use_gpu` defaults to **true**, so the GPU was requested and silently downgraded; ffmpeg exited 0 throughout. Fix in progress; first CUDA build failed at ffmpeg configure (`whisper >= 1.7.5 not found using pkg-config` — generated `whisper.pc` carries no CUDA link deps). **Next**: split the single giant `RUN` into two stages so the ~41min whisper.cpp layer caches instead of recompiling per ffmpeg attempt.
  - **No VAD model was in the image at all** — only `ggml-large-v3.bin`. With `vad_model` unset the filter cannot segment on speech and falls back to the `queue` window. Confirmed numerically: 66 boundaries carried a 4ms/24ms gap vs 67 predicted by `duration ÷ queue`; all 7 boundary word-duplications sat on those seams, **zero** on exact chains. So the SRT mixes three boundary kinds — 162 ASR splits, 66 mechanical window seams, 24 real pauses — and only the last is speech-derived. `ggml-org/whisper-vad` is **MIT, 885 KB**.
  - **A/B measured** (identical 60s slice, CPU image): VAD on cuts 7 segments → 3 and roughly doubles length where it engages (1.9–3.6s → 4.4–5.6s), and kills sub-0.5s garbage fragments. It engaged on only ~1/3 of the slice, so the **aggregate median barely moved (2.48s → 2.58s) and would have led to the opposite conclusion.** Do not judge segmentation changes on medians over partially-affected files. Still short of the 17.1s soundbite; `vad_min_silence_duration` sweep is the open knob (0.5 is the default — switching VAD on is not tuning it).
  - **Trunk inventory (fan-out, 4 agents)** — real and shipped: `clap-embed` :8108 (`laion/larger_clap_music`, Apache-2.0, 512-dim); `analyze_beats.py` (librosa beat/key/MFCC/onset + KMeans over CLAP) → `beats_to_cgp.py` → `geometry.cgp.v1` → a2ui-renderer, with tests. **Genuinely absent**: clip/highlight/soundbite extraction (nothing takes `[t0,t1]` and cuts an excerpt) and AST/AudioSet/YAMNet/PANNs.
  - **Correction owed to the HF lane**: speaker diarization is **not** a model hunt. `pmoves/services/media-audio/server.py:165-178` already wires `pyannote/speaker-diarization-3.1` (MIT), alongside `whisper-large-v3-turbo` (MIT) and `superb/hubert-large-superb-er` (Apache). It is gated on **one env var, `HF_TOKEN`** — a secrets-funnel item. The container is **not running**. Caveat: it is turbo + transformers (file-in/JSON-out), not ffmpeg's native whisper filter, so for clip-point precision the lanes are not interchangeable.
  - **Defects found for other lanes** (not fixed in this lane, flagging so they are not re-discovered): (1) `.claude/mcp.json` declares Cipher at `/api/mcp/sse` → **404**; the server serves `/mcp/sse` → 200. Cipher is healthy at 6.1d uptime with **0 connections / 0 sessions / 0 events ever** — the memory layer is up and has never been spoken to. `CIPHER_API_TOKEN` is unset in the container, so the endpoints are unauthenticated and mcp.json's 401 note does not match runtime. (2) **`a2ui-renderer` has no compose service definition in any of the 44 compose files**, despite real code, a real Dockerfile, and `pmoves/Makefile:3796` deploying it — `make up-a2ui-renderer` would fail. (3) `ffmpeg-whisper` pins CUDA **12.6.2** and builds from bare `nvidia/cuda` while the fleet standardized on **12.8.1** with a repo-owned `pmoves/cuda-base` — drift.
  - **Dead ends, recorded so they are not retried**: NGC/`nvcr.io` hosts nothing for ffmpeg/whisper (adopted only for one NIM image + Jetson provisioning). DHI was attempted and **reverted in the same PR (#1084)** — `dhi.io` returned 401 to runners, Chainguard free tier was Python 3.14 only, and its own manifest lists `nvidia/cuda` as non-migratable.
  - **Register hygiene, for DARKXSIDE**: Mavis's `Lane 3 — supabase-stack-default-up` CLAIM (2026-07-31) has **no RELEASE** — 4 days open. Verify with Mavis before anything touches Supabase rather than assuming it landed. Separately, `AGNOTE4482_SITREP.md` self-declares "Last refreshed 2026-07-19" and is **~16 days stale** while `CLAUDE.md` lists it Tier-1 always-load cold-start orientation.

## MAI-UI serving stabilization — and a corrected read of the crawl gap — 4090 (2026-08-07)

- `2026-08-07T02:00:00Z` CLAIM `4090-claude (field)` branch: `feat/mai-ui-serving-service` (PR #2468) · scope: **MAI-UI serving stabilization only.** Narrowed on review — the original wording claimed "close the research/crawl hole," which over-reserved a lane this work does not close. **The crawl-gap lane is explicitly left OPEN for any agent.** Recorded late: this entry was written 2026-08-07 and pushed to `docs/claim-crawl-gap-lane` but not PR'd until 2026-08-08, so it provided no collision protection during the work it describes. Kept as a record, not presented as a live reservation.

  **Correction to this entry's first draft, on review by Codex.** The original said Agent Zero's "full MCP registry" has "no fetch, no crawl." That is false. `pmoves/services/agent-zero/mcp_server.py:653` registers `e2b.surf.scrape` — "Scrape/web surf a URL and extract content (url, depth, extract_content, follow_links)" — implemented at `:567`, dispatched at `:718-719`. The command exists.

  **What is actually broken is its backend, and that is a sharper finding than the original.** `e2b_surf_scrape` POSTs to `{E2B_MCP_SERVER_URL}/surf/scrape`, defaulting to `http://e2b-mcp-server:7073` (`mcp_server.py:61`). That service is (a) **not a service in `pmoves/docker-compose.yml`**, (b) **not running on this node**, and (c) the `pmoves-e2b-mcp-server` fork — which does have a root `Dockerfile` — contains **no `surf` route at all**; it is a pnpm/TypeScript workspace with no HTTP endpoint of that shape. So the command is registered and dispatchable, and the endpoint it dispatches to was never built anywhere in the repo. A caller gets a connection error, not content.

  **Archon's side, unchanged and independently verified.** `archon.crawl.request.v1` / `archon.crawl.result.v1` are registered in `topics.json` with closed schemas and the ARCHON JetStream stream is live (#2397, verified on 5090). `orchestrator.py::_handle_crawl_request` → `_process_crawl` exists (restored in #2464, 39/39 tests green). But `_process_crawl` does not crawl: it reads `fragments` and `extracted_text` out of the **incoming request** metadata and republishes them with `status: "completed"`. Re-verified against `origin/main` 2026-08-08. Repo-wide there is no crawler — zero `crawl4ai` / `firecrawl` / `scrapy` in any Python file. **A crawl request returns an empty result and reports success.**

  **So the gap is real but was mis-described.** It is not "Agent Zero has no fetch tool." It is two unbuilt backends behind two registered surfaces: `e2b.surf.scrape` → a route that does not exist, and `archon.crawl.request.v1` → a handler that echoes its own input. Anyone picking up the crawl lane should evaluate **completing the E2B surf backend** before building a parallel VL-fetch path, since the MCP command, the dispatch, and the fork's Dockerfile already exist.

  **What PR #2468 actually does (the whole of this claim):** (1) gives `mai-ui-agent/Dockerfile` a compose home — it was an ORPHAN_DOCKERFILE failing the #2417 ratchet; (2) adds a `MAI_UI_BACKEND=local|remote` switch, since `MODEL_PATH` only chose which LOCAL snapshot to load, making "cloud variant on 4090, local on Spark" unreachable as written; (3) guards `torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)`, which sat inside a log call and raised before model load on any non-CUDA node. Serving stabilization. It does not wire `_process_crawl` to anything.

  Three-body: delivery=4090-claude, control=DARKXSIDE, memory=this trail + [[project_creator_operator_lattice]]. Deliberately out of scope: submodule sync + audit (z890-claude), the `archon.mint.*` family, and the crawl wiring itself. CHIT trail unsigned-local (no signing passphrase in this session). agent_signature: `ACK::4090-claude::MAI-UI-SERVING-CLAIM-2026-08-07`.

## Skill-check Danger Room — verifiable artifacts — HANDOFF 4090 → SPARK (2026-08-08)

- `2026-08-08T18:40:00Z` HANDOFF `4090-claude (field)` → `SPARK` scope: **Tier-2 skill verification — exercise skills in a sandbox and emit verifiable artifacts.** Handoff doc: `pmoves/docs/handoffs/SKILL_DANGER_ROOM_VERIFICATION_SPARK_2026-08-08.md`. Substrate: `PMOVES-E2B-Danger-Room` (E2B fork, populated, `templates/base` + `spec/openapi.yml` + 4 SDKs; **gitlink currently drifted `+` — sync before building**). **Not claimed by 4090** — SPARK is the named owner; this row assigns the lane and leaves it open for SPARK to CLAIM.

  **The gap, precisely.** Today's ratchets (#2488, #2494) made every *named* thing checkable: a skill's `make` targets, `file:NNN` citations, ssh hosts, and the damage-control guard's own routing table all now resolve or fail a gate. **None of that proves a skill does what it claims.** A skill can be perfectly anchored and completely wrong. The ladder: **T1 anchored** (static, shipped) → **T2 exercised** (Danger Room, this handoff) → **T3 receipted** (CHIT-signed, reflected across nodes, designed here and follows T2). T1 without T2 is a spell-checked lie; T2 without T3 is a result only the running node can trust.

  **Why SPARK, on capability not availability.** DGX GB10 Grace-Blackwell / 128 GB unified (`configs/claws/scopes/spark.json`, `role: gpu-inference`, `hostname: pmoves-spark`) — a skill claiming "route through TensorZero to a local model" can be *actually* exercised there; on a CPU node the same run is a mock, and a mock receipt is worse than none. `sign_trail: true` + `damage_control_hooks: true` are already set in its claw scope, so T3 needs no new trust wiring. And a sandbox resets free — skills get run against their *worst* fixture, not their friendliest.

  **The stage requested (5 pieces):** (1) a `pmoves-skill-check` sandbox template beside `templates/base`, **with submodules populated** — the `SUBMODULE_BUILD_AND_MOUNT_GAP.md` failure applies directly and an empty-submodule sandbox yields confident false negatives; (2) an **additive** skill frontmatter contract (`scope` / `nodes` / `danger_room.{fixture,artifacts,budget}`) where `scope` reuses the existing `validate_command_anchors` classifier vocabulary rather than inventing a second one — declared scope disagreeing with actual command scope is itself a finding; (3) fixtures that **plant a falsifiable failure**, versioned beside the skill, not happy paths; (4) an artifact contract that is deterministic, self-describing (carries the **skill content hash**, or the verdict cannot be tied to what it judged), signed-or-explicitly-labelled-unsigned, and addressable; (5) **CHIT reflection** — one `skill.verified.v1` receipt per run `{skill, skill_sha, fixture, node, verdict, artifacts[], wall_s, ts, sig, kid}`, registered as a closed schema **before** first publish (the `archon.crawl.*` family is the cautionary tale of a subject registered against an operation nobody built). Native per node, reflected in all: "works on spark, untested on 4090, failed on knuckles at fixture X" becomes a readable fact instead of folklore.

  **Why it matters beyond hygiene.** A 1B-param agent cannot judge whether a skill will work; it can *read a receipt*. The heavy model pays the discovery cost once in the Danger Room and leaves pre-digested context; the small model selects on it rather than reasoning about it. Same principle already proven by `patterns.yaml` — the guard hands over the correct path, valid parameters, and how to verify, so the model does not need to know PMOVES. Capability in the field, not the model. This extends that from "what to do when blocked" to "what actually works."

  **Acceptance, observable from artifacts alone (no need to watch a run):** template exists + sandbox has populated submodules (`git submodule status` shows no leading `-`); ≥1 skill declares the contract; ≥1 fixture plants a falsifiable failure; a run emits a self-describing artifact carrying the skill content hash; re-run is deterministic; **and a deliberately broken skill produces a RED artifact.** That last one is the acceptance test that matters and the one to cut last — every gate shipped on this node today was weaker than advertised until something proved it could say no.

  **Explicitly NOT in this handoff:** deploying `e2b-mcp-server` (Agent Zero registers `e2b.sandbox.*` / `e2b.spell.execute` / `e2b.surf.scrape` against `e2b-mcp-server:7073`, which is not a compose service, is not running, and for `surf` has no route in the fork at all — separate lane, must not block this one, and those MCP commands must not be assumed working because they are registered); retrofitting all 31 skills (two or three real fixtures beat thirty aspirational ones); building T3 before T2 emits something worth signing.

  Three-body: delivery=SPARK, control=DARKXSIDE, memory=this trail + the handoff doc + the receipts once T3 lands. Owned elsewhere: `pmoves/mk/infra.mk` (z890 #2480), WS4-B living-docs mesh (Mavis-5090). CHIT trail **unsigned-local** (no signing passphrase in this session). agent_signature: `ACK::4090-claude::SKILL-DANGER-ROOM-HANDOFF-SPARK-2026-08-08`.

## WS2 tooling audit RELEASE + lane sweep — 4090 (2026-08-09)

- `2026-08-09T00:30:00Z` RELEASE `4090-claude (field)` scope: **WS2 (z890 coordination plan) COMPLETE — all four claimed items shipped, plus two ratchets and one handoff the audit surfaced.** Claim was `2026-08-08T13:00:00Z` (TTL 72h); closing well inside it. **8 PRs, all merged:** #2482 (claim + handoff + 3 enumeration corrections), #2483 (ci-expedition skill — wrong trigger row, unrunnable reclaim block, new `_app-token` signature row), #2484 (the two `claude-pmoves.sh` were never duplicates — delegation, nothing deleted), #2485 (submodule build & mount gap runbook), #2486 (`up-*` inventory, 88 targets, zero Makefile edits), #2488 (`validate-command-anchors` ratchet), #2494 (first-contact + guard-routing-table coverage), #2495 (Danger Room handoff → SPARK). `pmoves/mk/infra.mk` untouched throughout — z890's #2480, no collision.

  **What the audit actually found, beyond the enumerated items.** (1) **A gate can advertise coverage it does not have** — three times: the compose-split drift gate existed but its path filter omitted the generator that produces the overlays, so it never fired on the PR that caused the drift (#2474); the anchor ratchet's first cut missed fenced code blocks, i.e. the *commonest* form in a runbook, and inline prose entirely (~15% of its own surface, #2488 review); and its baseline never checked for *stale* entries, so "count only goes down" was a claim with nothing enforcing it. Each was caught by review, not by me. (2) **`.claude/CLAUDE.md` — the always-loaded orientation file — cites `worktree-sitrep-strict` as "authoritative, prefer this."** It does not exist. Nor do `worktree-sitrep`, `tac-status`, `tac-check`, or `health-quick` (BOOTSTRAP.md). First contact for every agent entering this repo is misdirection. (3) **The damage-control guard's own routing table has two dead roads** — it offers two Make targets (the mini-cli reset road and the schema-migration road) as the "correct path", and neither is defined in the Makefiles, so a well-behaved agent that follows the offered road hits a wall at its least recoverable moment. (4) **`patterns.yaml` is the pattern worth generalizing** — 121 entries, each carrying why / correct path / valid parameter set / how to verify, with `ask: true` rather than a hard no. `.claude/hooks/pre-tool.sh` runs a flat 11-entry deny-list in front of it that names no road at all, and **5 of its 11 entries duplicate `patterns.yaml` minus the affordance**. Same "layer re-types instead of naming" defect as the four cleanup copies.

- `2026-08-14T00:00:00Z` CLAIM `CLAUDE-OPUS-5 (4090)` scope: **Pinned-without-an-upgrade-check, root-caused and fixed — plus the docs-accuracy ratchet that found it.** Operator directive (2026-08-14): *"needs to update pinned image without any upgrade check is what has causing issues"*. **Confirmed, with a precise mechanism.** `agent-zero-upstream-check.yml` reads the pin from `pmoves/services/agent-zero/Dockerfile.multiarch` (`ARG AGENT_ZERO_UPSTREAM_VERSION=v2.8`) and is supposed to open a bump PR when upstream moves. That step is gated `if: UPSTREAM_CURRENT == 'false'`, i.e. it runs **only when a bump is actually due**, so on every run where the pin was current it was skipped and the workflow went green. Upstream **v2.9 was tagged 2026-08-12 14:51 +0200**; the 08-12 run fired at 06:00 UTC (before the tag) and passed, and the runs on **08-13 and 08-14 both failed** — the first two times the step has ever executed. `create-pr` `needs:` that job, so it was skipped. **The bump PR this workflow exists to open has never been openable.** Fix: add `GH_TOKEN` to the step (**PR #2542**). - `2026-08-15T06:45:00Z` CLAIM `Mavis (orchestrator, mvs_5d5493b128b640e9aff8d45adcc77a66)` scope: **Post-merge fix for the 4 codex-flagged P1 bugs in PR #2568's workflows** (the `branch-protection-drift.yml` and `branch-protection-ruleset-sync.yml` files merged into main on 2026-08-15). **Why this slice exists:** my 17/17 unit tests in PR #2568 all passed; CI was green; the operator admin-merged. The codex automated review found 4 P1s post-merge that NO unit test could have caught — they were workflow-glue bugs (the Python tool was fine; the YAML+bash wiring was broken). **The 4 P1s:** (1) `branch-protection-ruleset-sync.yml` apply loop passed the repo POSITIONALLY and added a NONEXISTENT `--dry-run` flag — every invocation was a silent no-op (arg-parse error). The apply never actually applied. (2) `branch-protection-drift.yml` had no GitHub App token mint, so `drift_check()` 401s on every repo and reports each as `audit_error` — the daily cron was actively publishing FALSE drift. (3) The drift pipeline used `set -e` without `pipefail`, so the `tee` step's 0 exit masked publisher failures — the workflow reported success on a broken publisher. (4) The resolve step wrote `repos.tsv` but never defined a `repos` step output, so the App-token action received an empty `repositories` input — with `owner` set, that escalates to EVERY accessible repository (privilege escalation + slow). **Plus one I caught myself before codex:** the apply loop's `if python | tee; then APPLIED else FAILED` branch also relied on `tee`'s 0 exit (no `set -e`, but the same trap), so FAILED was never incremented. Fixed with `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` instead. **What lands in this slice:** (a) `branch-protection-drift.yml` — add `actions/create-github-app-token` step (with `owner: ${{ github.repository_owner }}`, `permission-contents: read` — no admin needed for read-only drift), pass `GH_TOKEN: ${{ steps.app-token.outputs.token }}` to the drift step, change `set -e` to `set -e -o pipefail` so publisher failures surface. (b) `branch-protection-ruleset-sync.yml` — change the resolve step to ALSO write a `repos<<__EOF__ ... __EOF__` heredoc to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` (load-bearing: empty output = every accessible repo), so the App-token step can scope to the resolved set; fix the apply loop to `args=("--repo" "$repo")` + conditional `--no-dry-run` (only when `DRY_RUN=false`); switch from `if pipeline; then` to `PIPESTATUS[0]` capture so FAILED actually counts. (c) `tests/test_branch_protection_workflows.py` — 14 regression tests across 4 groups (ApplyArgsTests, DriftAuthTests, PipefailTests, HeredocOutputTests) that parse the YAML + grep the right patterns. **These tests would have caught all 4 bugs if they had existed pre-merge.** (d) `pmoves/tools/LEARNINGS/branch-protection-v0_LEARNINGS.md` — new lesson #12 ("Workflow glue needs code review, not just unit tests") + new 5-class taxonomy row + lesson count 9 -> 12. **Verification:** 14/14 regression tests pass; the 4 P1s confirmed by reading the fixed workflows end-to-end; CI green; the 2 workflows now match the patterns in the existing `branch-protection-sync.yml` (which has them right — I just didn't copy the patterns over). **Critical pattern captured:** the 17/17 unit tests passed but the workflows were broken. The 14 regression tests assert on the workflow-glue patterns (positional vs flag args, App token mint, pipefail, output export) that a unit test of the underlying Python could not have caught. Unit tests cover the Python. Workflow files need a separate test class. This is a corollary to lesson #9 (the test that can only assert absence): the 17/17 was a *test that could only assert the Python was correct*, not that the workflow invoked it correctly. **Three-body:** delivery=Mavis, control=DARKXSIDE (operator admin-merges this fix), memory=this trail + the 14 regression tests + the LEARNINGS file. CHIT trail unsigned-local. `agent_signature: ACK::Mavis::BRANCH-PROTECTION-WORKFLOWS-FIX::2026-08-15`.

- `2026-08-15T16:30:00Z` CLAIM+RELEASE `Z890-CLAUDE` scope: **Fleet-pass cluster delivered as 7 atomic PRs; 4 merged by operator, 2 open, 1 re-land opened after a squash-merge silently dropped a verified fix.** **(T1 — bounded JuiceFS cache, PR #2501, merged `092843a90`)** JuiceFS ships host-blind cache defaults (`--cache-size` 100 GiB in `/var/jfsCache` + `--free-space-ratio 0.1`); the 0.1 guard **self-disables caching** once the cache FS drops below 10% free, so on a near-full node (SPARK ~17 GiB free) *every* read streams from tailnet MinIO — fatal for a 26 GiB encoder that reloads per generation. Same class as #2452: **disk is a host property — measure it, don't assume.** New `pmoves/scripts/juicefs-cache-bounds.sh` df-measures the target cache FS and emits bounds sized to *actual free* (never a constant), dropping `free-space-ratio` **below** 0.1 on a near-full disk so caching stays ON (17 GiB → 0.017, verified). Wired into all three call sites: `mk/egress.mk juicefs-mount-local`, `scripts/juicefs-cross-node-setup.sh`, `docker-compose.yml juicefs-gateway` (in-container measurement at start, since compose renders host-side). **Units settled empirically, not by reading `--help`:** stood up a throwaway Postgres + real `ce-v1.3.0` format+mount — `--cache-size 8704` reports `.config CacheSize=9126805504` (= 8704×1024²), proving a bare integer is MiB, which the entire guard depends on. **(T1 review)** 4090-CLAUDE pair-reviewed and pushed `996e44d0f` with 4 items; **two were bugs I missed and adopted**: (a) `read … || true` — `set -e` killed the script on df-failure EOF *before* my graceful fallback could run, so the safety net was **dead code on exactly the path it existed for**; (b) `docker-compose.juicefs.yml` is **generated** via `make compose-split` and my source-only edit left it stale. Both fixed. Then **both of us independently found the same further bug** (their "New P2"): a `df` device name containing a space (Git Bash reports `/` as `C:/Program Files/Git`) shifts positional `$2/$4` → non-numeric total, which is **non-empty** so it passes the `[ -z ]` guard, and POSIX awk **string**-compares `total_kib > 0` → takes the division branch → `division by zero`. Silent half: `$4` is *Used*, not Available, so cache would be sized off used space (687G vs 239G). **(T2/T3 — PR #2502, merged `951520273`)** `B850_BRINGBACK_RUNBOOK.md` closes the "no checklist exists" gap for re-enrolling B850 (the **single data-tier home** — do not split-brain Postgres/NATS): resolve-hostname-first (3 drifted names: `pmoves-b850-ai-top` authoritative vs `pmoves-9850x3d-r9700` vs `pmoves-rdna4`), CHIT enroll, `tailscale up --advertise-tags=tag:pmoves,tag:gpu,tag:lab` per `tailscale-acl-policy.json` (**not** the z890 script's tags), the measured don't-untag regression (untagging drops a node out of `tag:gpu` → fleet Ollama :11434 unreachable, as happened to pmoves-4090), SSH-is-by-tag (no key install), and **two HIGH security follow-ups**: re-create `juicefs-mount` with `META_PASSWORD` (the Supabase admin password sat inline in `ps`/`docker inspect` for days) and **rotate** it. `JUICEFS_CROSS_NODE_MOUNT_RUNBOOK.md` canonicalizes the **minio-backed `pmoves`** volume for 4090/5090 (NOT file-backed `pmoves-media`, which lists filenames then I/O-errors every read). **(T4 — PR #2502)** `MINIMAX-H3-*` installer (`.bat` + RunPod `.sh`), parametric on `COMFY_ROOT` so it targets either the in-tree PMOVES-Creator fork (H3 nodes **native** at `comfy_extras/nodes_minimax_h3.py`) or the operator's Pinokio fork; clones only the 3 aux packs. HF sources byte-verified via `hf_fs`. **Two brief premises corrected by recon:** (1) loader filenames are **free** (ComfyUI dropdowns) — the `minimax_h3_fl2va_pruned_int8_convrot` name traced to a different, pruned-W4A8 repo and is unverified, so no rename machinery; (2) the layers-50–63 "tail" is **NOT** the conditioning encoder — H3 consumes the hidden state *after layer 49*, so the **24.55 GiB layers-0–49 file is REQUIRED** and the brief's "~19 GB saving" was wrong (working set 55.5 GB, not ~37). **Flagged for operator decision:** the only available encoder is an **abliterated/uncensored** fine-tune (no stock Qwen3-VL H3 encoder exists) — material for UNFCU/client-facing work; and **NVFP4 has never been run end-to-end on a 5090** (architecture-based rating only). **(Landing sweep)** Re-checked the "uncommitted pile" against `origin/main` rather than the stale local branch: most of it was **already merged** (sso #2454, traefik #2453, nats-mcp #2496) and only *looked* uncommitted. Real new work split into targeted PRs: **#2514** storage runbooks (open), **#2515** 3 coordination handoffs (merged `a3ee31b16`; z890 Tailscale IP **redacted to hostname** per the no-literal-IPs rule), **#2516** value-engine-domino v0 spec (open). Deliberately excluded with reason, not blind-committed: `secrets_manifest.yaml` (damage-control **zero-access** — lands via the CHIT pipeline's own gated flow), `PMOVES-mike-local/` (nested gitignored repo), `settings.json` (key-order churn), and **~20 submodule gitlink bumps** (operator-directed to a dedicated verified pass — blind-committing pointers is the drift trap). **(Post-merge finding → PR #2574)** After the operator merged #2501, verification against main showed the spaced-`df` fix **absent**: the squash captured the branch at `996e44d0f`, so `ae98c4783` never reached main (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` false). Reproduced the crash against main's own copy of the helper (`awk: fatal: division by zero attempted`, EXIT=1) and re-landed it as **#2574** (single file, ~15 lines): right-anchored `$(NF-4)/$(NF-2)` parse + non-numeric coercion, with the numeric contract documented in the header. Verified: crashing invocation now EXIT=0 emitting correct bounds and reading **238.4 GiB free = df `Avail`**, not `Used`; survey/container-path/near-full paths unchanged. **Lesson: a squash-merge can silently drop a post-review commit — verify the fix is an ancestor of main, don't trust the merged-PR status.** **Three-body:** delivery=Z890-CLAUDE, review=4090-CLAUDE (pair review on #2501, convergent independent discovery of the df-column bug), control=DARKXSIDE (merged 4 PRs; owns B850 enroll + password rotation + node mounts + 5090 install + the encoder-provenance call), memory=this trail. **Operator lane still open:** B850 enroll/security follow-ups, 4090+5090 `pmoves` mounts, 5090 H3 install + the 3 reconciliation inputs (SEAP `.bat`, workflow JSON, mirror link). CHIT trail unsigned-local. `agent_signature: ACK::Z890-CLAUDE::FLEET-PASS-JUICEFS-B850-H3::2026-08-15`.

The harness v0 follow-ups publisher + the Mavis orchestrator's cross-boundary dispatch call this for model access. (b) `Pmoves-minimax-cli` (upstream `MiniMax-AI/cli`) - the CLI wrapper around the same MCP, used by the sidecar lane (deployed via Pinokio on operator devices). (c) `Pmoves-MiniMax-Provider-Verifier` (upstream `MiniMax-AI/MiniMax-Provider-Verifier`) - the provider conformance gate. Validates that any third-party provider claiming MiniMax compatibility actually meets the contract (model list shape, response format, token accounting, tool-call support). A provider that passes the gate is allowed into the model cascade; a failure is a hard block. This is the load-bearing test for adding new providers. **Why these 3 are one PR, not 3:** they form a single tier (model surface). Splitting them would mean the MCP ships without a CLI to consume it from the sidecar, and without a verifier to gate new providers. The 3-PR strategy already gives the operator 3 layers of revert granularity (skills / model-cascade / agents.md format); splitting each PR further is over-engineering. **Out of scope (intentional, lives in other PRs of the fold-in):** PMOVES-skills (PR1, MERGED); PMOVES-agents.md (PR3); the deprecated pre-recenter Pmoves-Minimax-skills URL (preserved as a README reference in PR1, not a separate submodule). **Verification:** 3 stacked commits (`33c5ae608a` feat submodule wire, `9a63212829` docs context+registry, pending the third docs AGNOTE+LEARNINGS); `python -c json.load(...)` passes on the registry; 3 submodules at the upstream HEAD (MCP=`f4d6a61b`, CLI=`24875e0f`, Provider-Verifier=`53d2d8a0`); the 3 entries land in their semantic sections (MCP in "MCP Tools & Extensions", CLI+Provider-Verifier in "Development Tools"). **Three-body:** delivery=Mavis, control=DARKXSIDE (operator reviews + admin-merges), memory=this trail + the per-PR LEARNINGS file. ## Service bring-up + agent-tier restore (2026-08-18) — Z890-CLAUDE — LANE BOARD

- `2026-08-18T14:30:00Z` CLAIM+RELEASE `Z890-CLAUDE` scope: **Data tier restored → agent tier unblocked; lanes opened for parallel claim.** **What was actually broken:** `pmoves-supabase-kong-1` had crash-looped **3,924 times**, and since Kong is the REST gateway on :8000, everything behind it was dark — including Archon, which sat reporting `"status":"initializing" / "credentials loading..." / ready:false` for **three days** while still looking `healthy` to Docker. Three stacked faults, each hiding the next: **(1)** `DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` was absent from `env.shared` and every tier file (compose passed empty → Kong refused the basicauth entry) **and was not even listed in the funnel's `Missing secrets (non-fatal)` warning**, which is why it went unnoticed for thousands of restarts; minted via the `secrets-rotate` Known Road. **(2)** Upstream commit `9777f051d6` (#45462 "restrict rest root anon") added a route using the **expressions-router** syntax (`expression: http.path == "/rest/v1/"`); under the default traditional router that is an unknown field and **one bad route fails the ENTIRE kong.yml parse**, taking all 21 services with it. Upstream sets `KONG_ROUTER_FLAVOR: expressions` beside that route (`PMOVES-supabase/docker/docker-compose.yml:95`) but PMOVES defines its **own** supabase-kong service and never carried the var over — the submodule config (live-mounted, so it updates on every bump) outran our service definition. Counts: 1 route uses `expression`, 20 use `paths`. **(3)** `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`/`SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` — Supabase's NEW opaque key model (`sb_secret_...`) — were **aliased to the legacy JWTs**, so each Kong consumer declared the same key twice and Kong rejected on a uniqueness violation. Proven by sha256 fingerprint from the live container (values never printed): PUBLISHABLE==ANON, SECRET==SERVICE. Upstream ships both EMPTY and its entrypoint strips blank entries ("Remove empty key-auth credentials (unconfigured opaque keys)", `kong-entrypoint.sh:47`) — empty is the SUPPORTED state. Fixed at **three** sources so it cannot recur: `bootstrap/registry.json` (listed SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY as a canonical *alias* of SERVICE_ROLE_KEY — the seeding origin), `scripts/supabase/apply_env_profile.py:93` (rewrote it on every profile apply), and pinned empty in the kong service. **Result: Supabase 13/13 healthy, Kong restarts=0, REST verified (401 without key, 403 with anon on the restricted root = upstream intended behaviour).** **Agent tier:** Agent Zero `/healthz`→200; Archon recreated and now `status:ok`. Archon's failure was purely downstream of Kong — it could not load credentials while the gateway was down. Note Archon runs its **own** postgres (`pmoves-archon-postgres`), which name-conflicts on `--force-recreate`; bring it up without forcing. **secrets-audit fixed:** it used `REPO_ROOT.rglob("*")` and filtered afterwards; rglob cannot prune, so it descended into every `node_modules`/`.venv`/`vendor` across ~50 submodules and then READ+regex-scanned them — 400,000 paths in 6.2s and still unfinished. Replaced with pruning `os.walk` (walk now 3.8s; audit completes in 394s instead of never). **This is why `make secrets-funnel` "hung"** — it calls secrets-audit as step 6 of 7, and steps 1–5 (including the env writes) had already completed, so operators saw a command that never returned and reasonably assumed total failure. NOT a worktree/branch artifact as first suspected. Also fixed a **correctness** bug found while profiling: linked worktrees live in TWO places — `.claude/worktrees` (excluded) and top-level `.worktrees/` (**not**) — so 13,398 files / 235 MB of duplicate repo had every finding double-counted. **Lineage corrections (agent-verified, both read-only):** `PMOVES-Wealth` is a direct fork of **`firefly-iii/firefly-iii`** off upstream **main** (0 behind / 31 ahead, branch `PMOVES.AI-Edition-Hardened`) — an earlier claim of "a grumpydictator fork" was WRONG: `grumpydictator/firefly-iii` is only Firefly's Packagist package name, inferred from composer.json rather than from the actual fork parent. `PMOVES-supabase` is a genuine fork of `supabase/supabase` synced 2026-08-06; **13/13 services present, ZERO `build:` stanzas (all official upstream images), and Kong route table + entrypoint + edge-functions + vector.yml are live-mounted straight from the submodule** — the strongest evidence the stack is built *on top of* upstream rather than reimplemented, and also exactly how fault (2) reached us. The CLI path still exists deliberately (`supabase/config.toml`, `SUPABASE_RUNTIME=cli|kong|compose`, and `tools/supabase_runtime_guard.py` which actively blocks both runtimes running at once). Verified empirically that upstream's 7 unmounted init SQL scripts are NOT a gap: schemas (`_realtime`, `_supavisor`, `auth`, `storage`, `realtime`, `graphql`, `extensions`, `pgbouncer`), all 8 roles, the `_supabase` database and `_analytics` within it are all present — the pinned postgres image bundles them.

  **OPEN LANES (parallel-safe unless noted):**
  - **L1 — Agent-Zero fork sync.** Tracked branch `PMOVES.AI-Edition-Hardened` is **52 behind / 44 ahead** of `upstream/main`. 44-ahead **exceeds fork-sync's `ahead_max=20` guard, so `fork-sync.yml` will skip it as MANUAL** — needs the skill's Step-4 manual merge, then image rebuild (`ghcr-build-one IMAGE=agent-zero`) + recreate. The parent gitlink is **correct** (pins `8b3b05d1` = tip of the tracked branch); the local `+` is only this checkout sitting on `security/merge-forward-pathcontain-hardened`, whose work is **already merged into hardened**.
  - **L2 — Supabase image drift.** `logflare 1.30.3` vs upstream `1.43.1` and `vector 0.28.1` vs `0.53.0` are the worst (13 and ~25 releases behind); kong/db/studio are patch-lag.
  - **L3 — gotrue env gaps.** Upstream sets `GOTRUE_DISABLE_SIGNUP`, `GOTRUE_JWT_ISSUER`, the four `GOTRUE_MAILER_URLPATHS_*` and external-provider toggles that PMOVES does not carry; auth features are silently unconfigured rather than deliberately disabled.
  - **L4 — funnel / Known-Road reconciliation.** `secrets-audit` exits NON-ZERO on findings and there are live ones (placeholder `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`/`SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD` in tier files; `.example` keys missing from runtime), so the documented `make secrets-funnel` **still fails at step 6** even now that it terminates.
  - **L5 — env.shared residue (OPERATOR-ONLY).** Lines 119–120 still carry the stale aliases. `env.shared` is agent-zero-access by design and `bootstrap_env.py --rotate` refuses an empty value, so the pipeline has **no clear-a-key operation** — that gap is itself worth closing.
  - **L6 — JuiceFS cross-node mounts (BLOCKED).** `b850:5432` unreachable from z890 *and* nano-1; needs Postgres bound to the tailnet interface — a security decision, not a mechanical port-publish.
  - **L7 — NATS leafnode 7422** (PR #2576) — the jetson EDGE-leaf unblocker.

  **Dependency order that matters:** data tier → Kong → Archon credentials → agent tier usable (this session's chain, per #1834). **Three-body:** delivery=Z890-CLAUDE, verification=two read-only research agents (Wealth lineage, Supabase build audit), control=DARKXSIDE. CHIT trail unsigned-local. `agent_signature: ACK::Z890-CLAUDE::SERVICE-BRINGUP-AGENT-TIER-LANES::2026-08-18`.

That validator would have caught the mismatch above on its first run, which is the argument for it. Claiming Part 1 only — **Part 2 (multi-sig quorum) stays open**, since a quorum mechanism should not be designed by one lineage.

  **Expression that transcends language — the carrier already exists; corrected mid-session.** The operator asked for identity expressed *"in a way that transcends, like a space message"* because *"models don't speak english"*, then corrected this session's framing: **"it already exists — chit packets... that's how you can ping and check anyway once geo bus is running."** That correction is right and this session had it backwards, having surveyed `signing-card.v1` (whose `ml` half is **cryptographic attestation** — ssh fingerprint, gpg id, App installation — proof of authority, not expression) and concluded a carrier would need designing. It does not. `pmoves/tools/chit_encode_hook.py` already emits `cgp.v2`, and a CGP packet is precisely a language-independent self-description:

  - `hyperbolic_coords` — a **position** (x, y, curvature, radius): two identities can be compared by distance, in no language at all
  - `spectral_signature` — a 6-band spectrum
  - `dirichlet_weights` — a distribution over factual / conceptual / procedural / contextual / relational
  - `holographic_boundary` + `content_hash` — integrity

  So this declaration ships **as a CGP packet**, not only as prose: `pmoves/docs/logs/identity_claude_opus_5.cgp.json` (`content_hash 53d34f50e6636024`, coords `x=0.405995 y=-0.246562 r=0.475 k=-1.0`).
