## B850-CLAUDE juicefs cross-node lane + secrets-pipeline hardening (2026-08-23 → 08-24)

- `2026-08-24T04:40:00Z` CLAIM+RELEASE `B850-CLAUDE (Opus 5)` scope: **JuiceFS cross-node gates 1–3.5 on B850, plus four pipeline defects the lane surfaced.** **Recorded retroactively, and that is the first finding:** this work ran to completion without a prior CLAIM. Eight PRs and three live DB mutations on the data-tier host, unclaimed. The gateway says "claim work before edits" and I did not. **(1) Lane state:** gates 1 (scoped role) and 2 (LOGIN + mount cutover) were already applied — the 5090's level-set read them as pending because the check needs a DB password and it correctly declined to handle one; from inside the container the credential is already in the environment. Verified instead by live evidence: `juicefs_meta` holds an active session from the mount container. **(2) #2702 pg_hba — the gate the handoff never had.** The lane's stated safety property was "the scoped role is what keeps this from being a superuser surface". It is not: a scoped role changes which credential JuiceFS *uses*, not which is *accepted*. `host all all 0.0.0.0/0 scram-sha-256` admitted `pmoves` and `supabase_admin` (both rolsuper+bypassrls) from anywhere. Added tailnet rules admitting only `juicefs_meta`. Source-IP preservation was *checked*, not assumed — Docker's only MASQUERADE rules are container→outside, inbound is pure DNAT. Repo-managed because the image ships pg_hba in the container's **writable layer**, which Step 4's own recreate would have discarded. **JuiceFS's PostgreSQL best-practices doc recommends exactly this dedicated-user + scoped-hba pairing — vendor practice, not local invention, and the pairing was what the handoff omitted.** **(3) Step 2.5 REVOKE + #2707:** three `SECURITY DEFINER` functions owned by a rolsuper were EXECUTE-able by PUBLIC — so `juicefs_meta`, a LOGIN role now reachable from the tailnet, inherited superuser execution. Revoked PUBLIC and `anon`; migration added so a volume reset cannot restore it, since the grants are Supabase *default privileges* and were never authored. The `anon` question was answerable from **RLS**, not from `SUPABASE_KEY`: four of five tables have RLS on with zero anon policies and `publisher_audit` carries an explicit `anon_deny`, so the publisher must be service_role. **(4) Rotation:** `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD` rotated (LEN=64) + `supa-bootstrap-db` + 8 client recreates; 13/13 healthy, 31 sessions re-established. **Correction to my own earlier claim:** I said distinct password hashes proved distinct secrets — wrong, SCRAM salts per ALTER; `pmoves`/`supabase_admin`/`authenticator` share one credential, so there was never a cheap-vs-expensive rotation fork. **(5) #2706 — the rotation was silently partial for a year.** `credential_urlencoder.py` ran *before* `secrets-funnel-sync`, read the previous rotation's tier value, and republished it into a file compose loads **last**. postgrest/gotrue/storage came back 28P01 holding a correct `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`. The tell I walked past: a second funnel run healed it. **(6) #2705 + #2710:** `juicefs_meta`'s password was a hand-placed file under another user's home, referenced nowhere — no second node could mount. Registered as a CHIT slot; Codex caught that the manifest row alone reaches nobody without a `sync-secrets-local.yml` env row, which is the trap #2700 documented five lines above where the row belongs. **(7) #2709:** the credential now has an owner (Z890, Infrastructure Coordinator) and a vendor-sourced runbook; diffing against JuiceFS docs found `META_PASSWORD_FILE` unused, `sslmode=disable` becoming material at Step 4, metadata restores never tested, and a single-server constraint that forecloses replication. **Village Rule, honestly:** I ran execution, control and memory bodies simultaneously. What actually caught four real defects was **Codex** acting as an unplanned control body — #2698 (I claimed a live Funnel grant was dead; my sweep used `--include=*.yml`, which does not match `*.yaml`, where both counter-examples live), #2702 (stale overlay), #2705 (workflow row), #2706 (encoder ordering), #2707 (role absent on fresh DB). The rule exists because a single agent reviewing itself misses these, and this session is the evidence, not the exception. **Recurring shape across every finding:** a fix that exists only as node-local runtime state — the exec bit, the pg_hba file, the juicefs cutover, the credential. The second node is what exposes them. **Open:** #2706, #2707, #2708 (5090's, fix pushed), #2709, #2710. Step 4 gated on #2702 merging; 5090 gated on the `JUICEFS_META_PASSWORD` Prod secret, which is Z890's per #2709. **Three-body:** delivery=B850-CLAUDE, control=DARKXSIDE + chatgpt-codex-connector, memory=this trail. CHIT trail **unsigned-local**. `agent_signature: ACK::B850-CLAUDE::JUICEFS-LANE-SECRETS-HARDENING::2026-08-24`.

Replacing it with a **node steward** that holds node context, files the CLAIM, and spawns delivery agents for the actual work. Generic `node-steward` agent resolved against node identity at runtime, NOT a per-node file duplicated seven times. **Identity — resolved as continuity, not a new name.** The operator offered either an alt identity or folding what was learned into the existing node slot. Folding in: the register carries 49 `B850-CLAUDE` references and the fleet convention is `<NODE>-<MODEL>`, so a second name would mean anyone reading B850's history has to know both. `KEYSTONE` survives as the *role descriptor* inside the agent — the through-line of the preceding lane was four instances of one defect class, a fix existing only as node-local state (launcher exec bit, pg_hba in a container writable layer, the juicefs cutover in a hand-run container's argv, the credential in a stray home directory), and making local truth bear load across the fleet is what the steward is for. The lessons belong in the agent definition so the next session on this node starts with them instead of rediscovering them; that is the actual deliverable, and a name was never it. **Village Rule, applied rather than noted:** the steward's purpose is to arrange the three bodies instead of being all three — the failure mode recorded in the entry above. **3-body:** delivery=B850-CLAUDE, control=DARKXSIDE + chatgpt-codex-connector, memory=this trail. The overlay's header taught the raw `-f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.amd.yml` chain — the path Known Roads steers away from and `known-roads-enforcer.py` exists to block — so it was reachable only by the discouraged path, on the exact node class it was written for. **The precedent was already in the tree and the overlay's own header named it**: `up-voice-amd` is the `-amd` sibling of `up-voice` and appends `docker-compose.amd-voice.yml` to the same chain; `up-ffmpeg-whisper` had no sibling. - `2026-08-28T01:15:00Z` RELEASE `B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)` scope: **Closes the 23:50 CLAIM — `pmoves/docs/services/pmoves-yt/RUNBOOK.md` (642 lines), plus a pointer from `README.md`.** **The two-paths fact held, and verification sharpened it in two ways that change operator guidance.** (1) **The image name is a hint, not the fact.** `docker-compose.integrations.images.yml` overrides *only* `image:` — the base service's `build: {context: ../PMOVES.YT}` **survives the merge**, confirmed by `docker compose config` on both chains. So a node with the submodule checked out can run a locally-built fork image *wearing the GHCR name*. The runbook therefore gives three commands, not one, and names `/healthz`'s `yt_dlp_version` as authoritative. (2) **`RepoDigests` does NOT discriminate build-vs-pull** — this node's locally-built `pmoves-pmoves-yt` carries `sha256:bf62dae4...`; I checked before writing it up rather than after. **This node is on the SOURCE path**: image `pmoves-pmoves-yt` (no registry prefix), `/healthz` → `2026.07.04`, matching the pin `ebd39b7f` — which `git -C PMOVES.YT branch --contains` places on the unmerged `feat/upstream-sync-2026.07`, NOT the `.gitmodules`-declared `PMOVES.AI-Edition-Hardened` (`2026.02.04`). The `--remote` downgrade hazard is real and documented with the reproduction. **Two undocumented failure modes found while verifying, both in the "healthy but lying" class.** (a) **`pmoves-yt` on this node is running cookie-less**: `YT_COOKIES` reads the base-compose *placeholder* `darkxside.youtube.cookies.txt`, that file does not exist (dir holds only `.gitignore`), and no `yt-cookies-vol` is mounted — so the `yt-cookies` overlay was never applied. - `2026-08-28T15:42:00Z` RELEASE `B850-CLAUDE (Knuckles)` scope: **Closes the 2026-08-27T23:50 CLAIM (`docs/pmoves-yt-runbook`) — the substantive record is already the RELEASE at line 2289 (delivered as PR #2801, merged `cb676a9cc`); this row exists only to close the lane the register's own hook still reports open.** Re-derived through the parser (not by inspection): `claim-collision-pre.py`'s `open_claims_in()` treats a RELEASE that names ANY lane as narrowing scope to *only* those lanes (the "closes only named lanes" branch), vs. a bare RELEASE closing everything the owner holds. The RELEASE at line 2289 is not bare — its prose backticks `` `feat/upstream-sync-2026.07` `` while describing which branch the `PMOVES.YT` pin actually sits on (an **unrelated** submodule branch, mentioned incidentally, not the lane being released). `LANE_RE` matches it (conventional-commit prefix `feat/`, survives the `FILE_SUFFIXES` filter since `.07` isn't a file extension), so `lanes_in(line 2289)` returns `{feat/upstream-sync-2026.07}` — non-empty — and the release narrows to closing *that* string, never touching `docs/pmoves-yt-runbook`. Confirmed by running `open_claims_in()` against the live register: line 2285's CLAIM still reports open after 2289. **This is a durable grammar trap, not specific to this entry**: any RELEASE whose prose backtick-quotes *any* conventional-commit-prefixed branch name — including ones mentioned only to describe unrelated state, like a submodule's pin — silently narrows what that RELEASE closes. The register's own convention (99/120 historical RELEASE lines name no branch = full handoff) is what protects against this in practice; the trap fires specifically when an agent writes detailed, evidence-dense prose (this register's house style) that happens to cite another branch. **Disposition, not silence**: not touching the hook from this session — append-only scope, and a narrow fix keyed to this one sentence shape (e.g. excluding branch mentions inside "unmerged `X`" qualifiers) would be over-fit and could hide the next occurrence in a different phrasing. The durable mitigation is agent-side: a RELEASE line should backtick-quote branch-shaped tokens *only* for the lane(s) being closed, or should use `Closes lane(s): ` phrasing the parser's `BRANCH_MARKER_RE` already privileges. CHIT trail signed: `hmac 78wrZBik4W7gU3q2ZDvC+n79i8mPiaTu3/HcO61TJBI=`, kid `chit-signing-v01`, card `...036`. `agent_signature: ACK::B850-CLAUDE::PMOVES-YT-RUNBOOK-LANE-CLOSURE-RELEASE::2026-08-28`.

- `2026-08-28T23:40:00Z` CLAIM `Z890-CLAUDE` branch: `docs/claim-cipher-token-model` · **TTL 72h (expires `2026-08-31T23:40:00Z`)** · scope: **Cipher per-agent token model is unsound in a way that must be fixed before Phase C fleet rollout, not after.** Filed as a claim rather than a PR because the fix touches the auth schema and the mint surface, and because my first two readings of this code were WRONG — recorded below so the next agent does not repeat them. **Findings (verified in `Pmoves-cipher/src/pmoves/auth.ts`, not inferred):** (1) **The stored row IS the bearer.** Mint builds `token = cipher_{uuid.hex}` and stores that same `token_uuid`; `auth.ts:60-68` strips the prefix, reformats the hex as a UUID, and looks up `cipher_agent_tokens?token_uuid=eq.<uuid>&revoked_at=is.null`. There is no hash column in `20260728100000_cipher_agent_tokens.sql`. So read access to that table reconstructs every agent's token as `cipher_` + hex — and the migration brief's claim that the secret is *"returned ONLY at mint time (never stored in plaintext)"* is false. (2) **`scripts/mint_cipher_token.py` prints the secret twice** — the raw Supabase response (line 53) and `CIPHER_TOKEN=` (line 58) — both to stdout. This is why the mint Known Road cannot be run inside an agent transcript; I declined to run it for exactly this reason after leaking the bootstrap token earlier in the same session by a careless presence-check. (3) **Unvalidated input reaches the PostgREST filter** — `auth.ts:62-64` formats `uuidHex` only when its length is exactly 32; otherwise it passes through raw into `?token_uuid=eq.${uuid}&...` with no `encodeURIComponent` and no UUID validation. A token of the form `cipher_<not-32-chars>` therefore injects directly into the query string. **NOT verified as exploitable** — flagged as a surface, deliberately not probed against a live service. (4) **Bootstrap mode grants the full scope set under a shared identity.** A token NOT starting with `cipher_` is string-compared against `CIPHER_API_TOKEN` and resolves to agentId `bootstrap` with all six scopes. `make secrets-rotate` generates a `random_urlsafe` value, which has no prefix — so the rotation performed this session put this node in bootstrap mode, sharing one identity fleet-wide rather than being per-agent scoped. Correct for today, wrong to assume otherwise. **Method correction, recorded because it caused two wrong conclusions:** `git grep` searches the superproject index and does **not** descend into submodules. I twice concluded "nothing reads `cipher_agent_tokens`; the enforcement layer was never implemented" — both times because `Pmoves-cipher/` is a populated submodule my search never entered. The layer is fully implemented. Any claim of absence in this repo needs a submodule-aware search before it is trusted. **Prior art checked before claiming (per operator direction):** Archon mint contract review (#2332) is *agent* minting, a different lane, and its own §2 records "Implementation status: zero"; `chore/cipher-village-phase-b-support` is already merged as #2275 and is what introduced the mint script; PRs #2727/#2753 touch secret shape and role minting, not this. No open PR or branch claims this lane. **Proposed work:** store a hash and compare on lookup; stop printing the secret and hand it off via the `PMOVES_ROTATE_VALUE` env convention `secrets-rotate` already uses; validate/encode the uuid before it reaches the filter; and decide whether `bootstrap` should retain all six scopes. **Three-body:** delivery=Z890-CLAUDE (this node, Opus 5), control=DARKXSIDE + independent review, memory=this trail. CHIT trail identity resolved (`z890-claude`, display `Z890 Claude`, card `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000010`); **payload UNSIGNED — `CHIT_PASSPHRASE` not set on this node**, which BOOTSTRAP.md permits in dev and which is disclosed here rather than omitted. `agent_signature: CLAIM::Z890-CLAUDE::CIPHER-TOKEN-MODEL::Opus-5::2026-08-28`.

- `2026-09-03T10:30:00Z` CLAIM+RELEASE `Z890-CLAUDE` branch: `fix/guard-windows-path-spelling` · **TTL n/a — opened and closed in the same entry; nothing is held** · scope: **The register write-guard could be defeated by which slash you typed.** `_tokens()` calls `shlex.split(..., posix=True)` — correct for bash, and it treats `\` as an ESCAPE. So a Windows-spelled target is not merely tokenized oddly, it comes back with its separators DELETED (`C:\Users\me\t0\AGNOTE4482PHI.t1.md` → `C:Usersmet0AGNOTE4482PHI.t1.md`), leaving nothing for `os.path.basename` to find. Measured on Z890 (win32), same commands, same fixture, only the slash differs — `cp`, `dd of=`, `install`, `csplit -f`, `csplit --prefix=`, `split` all **ALLOW** (silent pass) with `\` and **BLOCK** with `/`; `mv`, `echo >`, `tee`, `sed -i` blocked either way because they are matched off the raw text rather than the token list. **Six write vectors on an append-only provenance file, decided by an operator's keystroke.** Second, platform-independent half of the same defect: `os.path.basename` does not treat `\` as a separator on LINUX, so a Windows-spelled path reaching a Linux runner misses there too — the guard has to answer identically wherever it runs. Fix: split on either separator, plus an `endswith` fallback for the case where the POSIX tokenizer already destroyed the separators. `endswith` deliberately OVER-matches, which is the safe direction for a guard, and its bound is pinned: `<REG>.bak/.orig/.rej/.BACKUP.123` — git's own merge-conflict artifacts, the scenario where a careful hand fixup is the whole recovery story — stay ALLOWED in both spellings. **How it surfaced:** a full Windows suite run, not a review. `test_claim_collision_hook.py` was failing 4 cases on this node while green in CI, because the suite builds paths with `tmp_path / REGISTER_NAME`, which yields `\` on Windows and `/` on Linux — so CI exercised one spelling and could never catch this. The 18 new cases hard-code BOTH spellings so a Linux runner can. Verified: 141 passed with the fix; with the hook reverted and the tests kept, exactly the 6 predicted cases fail and the 12 neighbour/allow cases still pass. **Also found, NOT claimed — routed to the `#2858` author:** that PR's branch is stale (58 tests where main has 123, so it predates the output-flag and cp-destination cases) and its rewritten hook returns `ask` where current main returns hard `BLOCK`, and `ALLOW` for `csplit`/`split` in BOTH spellings. It needs a rebase and a decision on whether `ask` is the intended contract before it lands; posted as a PR comment with the A/B table rather than acted on. **Also surveyed:** 9 open review comments across 6 PRs; `#2858`'s P1 is already fixed by its author via `_row_at()`, the remaining 8 belong to other nodes' lanes (notebook-mcp, cipher build pin, whisper arm64, wger, a0-archon skill) and were left alone. Three-body: delivery=Z890-CLAUDE, control=DARKXSIDE ("ensure tooling works on windows"), memory=this trail. pr_numbers: [this row]. risks: low — one function, over-matching in the safe direction, bound pinned by test. agent_signature: `ACK::Z890-CLAUDE::GUARD-WINDOWS-PATH-SPELLING::2026-09-03`.
